                            2. A.    05.15.18 
2.       RECOGNITION 
A.  TSD Mission Moment - Facilities Study Attendance Area  
                                        Review Team 
This year, the Troy School District formed a Facilities Study Team 
Attendance Area Review Committee to conduct a comprehensive review 
of current Troy School District attendance area patterns according to 
specific criteria . They were asked to look into resolving double split 
learning pathways, enrollment across buildings to address overcrowding 
and imbalances within the classroom, and to minimize changes to school 
attendance areas across Troy.  This team was made up of members of 
the community and our Pupil Accounting Coordinator.  The individuals 
volunteered and gave countless hours of their time and expertise to this 
important project. Tonight, we are pleased to recognize the members of 
the Facilities Study Attendance Area Review Team for their exemplary 
service. We thank them for their thoughtful and skilled analysis and the 
recommendations they shared that will help guide us as the District 
moves forward.   
We would also like to recognize and thank Deputy Superintendent for 
Teaching & Learning, Mark Dziatczak, and Assistant Superintendent, 
Elementary Instruction, Kris Griffor who were the administrative liaisons 
for this team.  The District could not have undertaken a comprehensive 
study like this without their leadership and direction.    
RECOMMENDED ACTION: Invite team members up and distribute certificates.

2. RECOGNITION 
B.  2017-18 Outstanding Support Staff Award    
The Troy School District has many outstanding support staff employees. This evening 
we are proud to recognize the accomplishments of 17 individuals who were nominated 
for the Troy School District Outstanding Support Staff Award. 
The nominees were chosen for this distinction by t heir colleagues, by parents and by 
former and current students, because they make a difference for our students each and 
every day. They exemplify our values and help us to ensure that when we say we ensure 
learning for all - ALL truly means ALL. 
We are grateful for their dedication and passion for our students. 
Would you please come forward as your name is called? (Distribute 
certificates/plants.)

District Outstanding Support Staff Employee of the Year. Congratulations, Tina!

                 2. D.     05.15.18 
2.       RECOGNITION 
 D.  National Teacher Appreciation Week 
Teacher Appreciation Week is observed nationally each year in May. This year, 
Teacher Appreciation Week was celebrated May 7th – May 11th.  At tonight’s 
meeting, the Troy School District and Board of Education would like to 
recognize all of our outstanding teachers by adopting the following resolution: 
WHEREAS, throughout Troy and across the country, teachers inspire, 
nurture and open children’s minds to new ideas,  
WHEREAS, teachers promote and strengthen the principles of democratic 
equality, develop leaders and encourage critical thinking; and 
WHEREAS, Troy teachers set the bar for excellence in education and 
continually exceed the highest standards in the profession; and 
WHEREAS, the goal of Teacher Appreciation Week is to raise national and 
local awareness of teaching excellence in building a better society; now,  
THEREFORE BE IT  RESOLVED, that the Troy School District  supports 
National Teacher Appreciation Week, May 7-11, 2018, and encourages our 
schools and community to appropriately recognize the vital and changing role 
of teachers in imagining and creating a better future for society. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Read and adopt resolution.

  Supported by:

other  people  in  order  to  make  sense  of  their  behavior 
and predict what they are going to do.  To celebrate the

Attendance Area Review 
Dr.  Machesky  shared  that  the  Facilities  Study  Team 
Summary  
Attendance  Area  Review  Committee  was  organized  to 
e n g a g e  i n  a  r e v i e w  o f  c u r r e n t  T r o y  S c h o o l  D i s t r i c t  
attendance  area  patterns  according  to  specific  criteria.  
The team was asked to look for ways to resolve double‐
split  learning  pathways  currently  within  District 
boundaries,  examine  enrollment  across  the  buildings  to 
address  overcrowding  and  imbalances,  and  to  minimize 
changes  to  school  attendance  areas  across  Troy.    Dr. 
Machesky  shared  the  committee’s  timeline,  meeting 
dates  and  the  key  concerns  in  attendance  areas.    The 
team provided some initial, potential options addressing 
the areas of concern.  The options were forwarded to the 
Transportation  Department  to  develop  initial  costs  and 
the  Central  Office  team  will  continue  looking  into  all 
options more closely.  An update will be provided to the 
Board  at  a  future  workshop  followed  by  a  final 
recommendation to be considered for implementation in 
the 2019‐2020 school year.

Mr.  Schmidt  announced  that  the  annual  Troy  Youth 
Assistance Youth Recognition Night is tomorrow evening 
beginning  at  6:15  p.m.  and  welcomed  everyone  to 
attend. 
Dr.  Machesky  shared  that  he  attended  the  IA‐East  Art 
Show and how great it was.  He also shared that several 
members  of  the  Superintendent’s  Student  Advisory 
Board will be participating in spring clean‐up at the Stage 
Day.  Dr. Machesky also announced the Troybery Awards 
which  will  begin  at  9:45  a.m.  a  week  from  Friday.    He 
encouraged students to keep focused on their studies as 
we approach the fourth quarter. 
Mr.  Schmidt  indicated  that  the  next  Board  Workshop 
Administration  Building  and  the  next  regular  Board 
Meeting will be held May 15th at 7:00 p.m. at the Services 
Building.  Mr. Schmidt also announced that there will be 
a  tour  of  the  Section  1  property  –  now  called  Turtle 
Rivers Cons ervancy and Stage Nature Center and invited 
the community to attend.  
Respectfully submitted, 
Gary N. Hauff 
Secretary, Troy Board of Education

5. CONSENT 
 D. CONSIDERATION:  Approve Oakland Intermediate School 
District 2018-2019 Budget 
  In accordance with Section 624 of the Revised School Code, as amended, 
the Troy Board of Education is required to review the proposed Oakland 
Intermediate School District budget and express either its support or 
disapproval of this budget.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Adopt the following resolution. 
  Moved by  
  Supported by  
WHEREAS: 
 1. Section 624 of the Revised School Code, as amended, requires the intermediate 
school board to submit its proposed budget no later than May 1 of each year to 
the board of each constituent district for review; and 
 2. Not later than June 1 of each year, the board of each constituent district shall 
review the proposed intermediate school district budget, shall adopt a board 
resolution expressing its support for or disapproval of the proposed intermediate 
school district budget, and shall submit to the intermediate school board any 
specific objections and proposed changes the constituent district board has to 
the budget. 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED THAT: 
 1. The Troy Board of Education has received and reviewed the proposed 
intermediate school district budget in accordance with Section 624 of the 
Revised School Code, as amended, and, by the adoption of this resolution, 
expresses its support for the proposed intermediate school district budget.  The 
Troy School District Board of Education believes that it is the responsibility of 
112
the Intermediate School District to be responsible for its own operations, 
including adoption of its budget.  The Troy School District’s s upport of the 
Oakland Intermediate School District’s budget is for the sole purpose of 
complying with Section 624 of the Revised School Code. 
 2. The secretary of the Troy Board of Education or his/her designee shall forward 
a copy of this resolution to the intermediate school board or its superintendent 
no later than June 1, 2018. 
 3. All resolutions insofar as they conflict with this resolution be and the same are 
hereby rescinded. 
Yes:  
No:  
Resolution declared adopted. 
 Secretary, Troy Board of Education 
The undersigned duly qualified and acting Secretary of the Board of Education of the 
Troy School District, Michigan, hereby certifies that the foregoing is a true and 
complete copy of a resolution adopted by the Troy Board of Education at a regular 
meeting held on May 15, 2018, the original of which resolution is part of the Board’s 
minutes, and further certifies that notice of the meeting was given to the public under 
the Open Meetings Act, 1976 PA 267, as amended. 
 Secretary, Troy Board of Education

District FTE
Fund Balance Target Setting Protocol 
 The proposed protocol for the development of each fund’ s year ending fund balance target will be predicated upon: 
 Known economic liabilities or designated reserves of a specific fund 
 A percentage of each fund’s operating expenditures as follows (policy change currently being discussed with Board of Education) 
 
 
 
 
 
 The fund balance target setting protocol recognizes the following: 
 The State of Michigan’ s economic environment 
 The financial condition of the LEAs  
 Maximize district support thru program support, subsidies and distributions

ISD Budget Resolution 
Revised School Code 380.624 - Overview 
1. Not later than May 1 of each year, the ISD shall submit proposed 
budget for next fiscal year to the board of each constituent district for 
review 
2. Not later than June 1 of each year, the board of each constituent 
district shall review the proposed ISD budget, shall adopt a board 
resolution expressing its support for or disapproval of the proposed 
budget 
3. Mail copy of resolution indicating support or disapproval (with 
rationale) to the Oakland Schools Board of Education

FY19 Tuition Programs 
 Oakland Opportunity Academy - Alternative High School program serves six districts in 
the SW Quadrant with enrollment of 190 FTE.  Tuition projected at $7,050 per pupil. 
 Virtual Learning Academy Consortium (VLAC) K-8 –  online program for K-8 students 
projecting enrollment of 260 FTE.  In-county tuition projected to be $6,450. 
 Virtual Learning Academy Consortium (VLAC) 9-12 –  online program for 9-12 students 
projecting enrollment of 35 FTE.  In-county tuition projected to be $6,700. 
 Oakland Accelerated College Experience (ACE) –  available  to qualifying 11th grade 
students, allowing them to earn up to 60 transferrable college credits; projecting 
enrollment of 170 FTE.  Per pupil tuition projected to be $4,900.

6.  PERSONNEL 
A.  CONSIDERATION:  Administrative Appointment – Finance Director 
I am pleased to recommend the appointment of Ms. Kandice Moynihan  to the 
position of Finance Director.  Ms. Moynihan has a comprehensive 15-year career 
in finance and accounting.  She began h er career following graduation from 
Wayne State University as an audit associate for Plante Moran, PLLC.   Ms. 
Moynihan then served as the Accounting Supervisor for Garden City Public 
Schools before accepting a position fr om Walled Lake Consolidated Schools as 
the Finance and Purchasing Manager . Ms. Moynihan is a Cer tified Public 
Accountant with the State of Michigan and Certified Financial Officer  with 
Michigan School Business Officials.  She served on the Executive Committ ee for 
the Oakland County School Business Officials along with being Presid ent for 
Oakland County P urchasing Officials.   Ms. Moynihan has a Bachelor of Arts in 
Business Administration from Wayne State University and a Master of Science in 
Accountancy from Walsh College.   
With your approval, Ms. Moynihan will begin her duties as Finance Director as of 
June 6, 2018.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Acceptance of the resolution below:  
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by         
 Supported by        
BE IT THEREFORE RESOLVED that the Board of Education issues a two -year 
probationary administrative contract without tenure in the administrative position 
to Ms. Kandice Moynihan, who will begin her duties as Finance Director as of  
June 6, 2018.

  6. B. 05.15.18 
6.  PERSONNEL 
B.  CONSIDERATION:  Administrative Appointment – Director, Employee  
        Services 
I am pleased to recommend the appointment of Mr. John Pagel to the position of 
Director, Employee Services.   Mr. Pagel started his career in education following 
graduation from the University of Michigan in 1989.  He began his education 
career teaching fourth and fifth grade with Algonac Community Schools.  In 
1996, Mr. Pagel went on to become an Assistant Principal at Avondale Middle 
School then in 2003 assumed additional responsibilities as the Director of 
Technology for the Avondale School District.  Mr. Pagel became the Principal at 
Avondale’s Deerfield Elementary in 2000 before accepting the position of Martell 
Elementary School Principal in 2016.  During his prestigious career at Avondale, 
he implemented and integrated more than twenty million dollars of IT software 
and professional development, participated in successful negotiation s of twelve 
District Collective Bargaining Agreement s serving both as a Board of Education 
team member and representing the unionized building administration 
association.  Mr. Pagel has a Bachelor’s Degree in Elementary Education from the 
University of Michigan and a Master of Education Educational Leadersh ip from 
Wayne State University.   
With your approval, Mr. Pagel will begin his  duties as Director, Employee 
Services as of July 1, 2018.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Acceptance of the resolution below:  
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by         
 Supported by        
BE IT THEREFORE RESOLVED  that the Board of Education issues a two -year 
probationary administrative contract without tenure in the administrative position 
to Mr. John Pagel , who will begin his duties as Director, Employee Services  
effective July 1, 2018.

OBJECTIVE 
To obtain the Director of Employee Services position where I can utilize my proficiency and 
experience to advance employee relations, services and the vision of the Troy School District. 
LEADERSHIP PHILOSOPHY 
My philosophy is that leaders should listen before they speak. They should earn the trust of those 
around them by exhibiting the traits of honesty, integrity, and compassion. Leaders empower 
those around them by creating a culture of collaboration and open communication. I strive to be 
this type of leader each day because I believe that we are always better together! 
LEADERSHIP EXPERIENCE 
Martell  Elementary School (Troy School District) - Principal 2016-Present 
 Responsible for over 450 Kindergarten - fifth grade students in a wonderfully diverse community. 
 Worked closely with the Parent Teacher Organization to maintain high levels of parental 
involvement. 
 Responsible for hiring, evaluating, mentoring, uniting and empowering all staff members to 
achieve the goal of creating a robust learning environment for our students. 
 Worked with staff to develop data driven grade level goals that align with our school and district 
improvement plans. 
 Developed a building Data Room and system for grade level quarterly student progress meetings. 
 Met regularly with students, parents, staff and local municipality to enhance building safety. 
 Designed a new master schedule that allowed for effective implementation of RTI principles. 
Deerfield Elementary School (Avondale School District) - Principal 2000-2016 
 Responsible for over 420 Kindergarten - fifth grade students. 
 Awarded National Blue Ribbon 2016 
 Designated Michigan Rewards School 2013 & 2014 
 Incorporated “Change Leadership” professional development model for building staff in 
partnership with Oakland Schools. 
 Worked closely with students, staff and parents to achieve 11 years of Adequate Yearly Progress, 
Grade A ratings and exemplary MEAP performance. 
Avondale School District – Human Resources Leadership Experiences 2000-2014 
 Avondale District Negotiations Team Member: Participated in the successful negotiation of 
twelve Teacher Collective Bargaining Agreements. 
 Experience and training in both traditional and Interest Based Bargaining (IBB) 
 Avondale Administrators’ Association Chief Negotiator 
 Chairperson for multiple hiring and interview committees. 
 Lead Administrator Liaison collaborating with Board of Education Attorneys to resolve 
AAA, AEA and AAES evaluation, discipline and contract issues. 
 Training and experience in the implementation of Five Dimensions (5D) and Danielson 
Framework teacher evaluation modules.

H.      CONSIDERATION:  Administrative Appointment – Principal, 
  Boulan Park Middle School 
I am pleased to recommend the appointment of Mr. Anthony Morse  to the 
position of Principal, Boulan Park  Middle School .   Mr. Morse started his 
education career as a Social Studies Teacher for the Stanwood Community 
Schools.  He then took a teaching position in 2002 with the Madison District 
Public Schools where he advanced his career to Principal/Athletic Director during 
the 2008 school year. Mr. Morse came to the Troy School District in 2011 as the 
Boulan Park Middle School Assistant Principal.   During his time at Boulan Park 
Middle School, he was an integral part of  implementing the professional growth 
models for all teaching staf f, creating the master schedule for all students and 
co-developing the Summer Success program for both incoming 9 th and 6th grade 
at-risk students.  Mr. Morse has a Bachelor of Science Degree in Education from 
Central Michigan University, Master in the Art  of Teaching from Marygrove 
College and an Education Specialist Degree from Oakland University.  
With your approval, Mr. Morse will begin his  duties as Principal, Boulan Park  
Middle School as of July 1, 2018.

10.  OTHER 
A.  CONSIDERATION:   Biennial Election for Oakland Schools Board of 
Education on June 4, 2018 
In accordance with Public Act 419 of 2004, the Troy Board of Education is 
required to elect board of education members to the Oakland Schools 
Board of Education and select a designate to represent the Troy Board of 
Education to cast a ballot on its behalf .  At this evening’s meeting, the 
Board will designate a representative to cast a ballot at the Oakland 
Schools Board of Education meeting on June 4 th and will then consider 
adopting a resolution as prescribed by the revised school code.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION :  Discussion of candidate, selection of TSD Board 
Representative and acceptance of the resolution below.   
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
Moved By: ______________________________ 
Supported By: ______________________________ 
RESOLUTION OF THE TROY BOARD OF EDUCATION 
WHEREAS, Public Act 419 of 2004 amended the election procedures for 
Intermediate School Districts that elect board members at a meeting of 
representatives from constituent district school boards, and 
WHEREAS, Public Act 419 of 2004 requires a constituent school board to designate 
its representative and identify the Intermediate School Board candidate the board 
supports by a resolution ado pted not earlier than 21 days prior to the date of the 
election, and 
WHEREAS, Public Act 419 of 2004 prescribes the method for passage of a 
resolution including the requirement to consider the resolution at not less than one 
public meeting before adopting the resolution, and 
WHEREAS, the Board previously considered the resolution at an open meeting 
conducted in a manner prescribed under the Open Meetings Act on May 15, 2018. 
210
  10. A.  05.15.18 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED THAT: 
1. The Board designates _____________________ __________ as its 
representative to serve on the 2018 electoral body responsible for electing 
members to the Oakland Schools Intermediate School District Board of 
Education. 
2. The Board supports candidate Dr. Theresa Rich  and candidate Mr. Marc Katz  
for the two (2) positions on the Oakland Schools Intermediate School District 
Board of Education each for a term of six (6) years ending June 30, 2024. 
3. The Board directs its representative _________________________________ 
to vote for candidate Dr. Theresa Rich and candidate Mr. Marc Katz at least on 
the first ballot taken at the June 4, 2018 election.

Oakland Schools Board of Education  
2018 Election Timeline 
March 12 – May 4, 2018:   
Candidates may pick up nominating petitions and affidavit forms at the Oakland County 
Clerk’s office, Elections Division, 1200 N. Telegraph Road, Dept. 417, Pontiac, MI 
48341. 
May 4, 2018 - 4:00 PM:  
Last day for candidates to file nominating petitions, filing fees, and affidavits of identity 
with the Oakland County Clerk’s office. 
May 7, 2018: 
Oakland Schools receives list of qualified candidates from Oakland County Clerk’s 
office. Oakland Schools sends list of qualified candidates to constituent district 
superintendents and board of education secretaries. 
May 14 – June 1, 2018:  
Each constituent district board of education must 1) designate its representative and 
identify the ISD candidates its board supports, and 2) consider and adopt a resolution 
affirming #1. Each board must consider its resolution at not less than one (1) public 
meeting before adopting the resolution.   
June 1, 2018 – 4:00 PM:  
Copies of district resolutions due to the Oakland Schools administrative office via fax 
248.209.2206 or email to Kathy.Boike@oakland.k12.mi.us. 
June 4, 2018 - 5:30 – 7:30 PM: 
District representatives attend Oakland Schools Board of Education open meeting to cast 
ballots on behalf of their districts.  No absentee ballots can be accepted. 
June 5, 2018: 
Notification of election results sent from Oakland Schools to district superintendents and 
Oakland County Clerk. 
July 17, 2018: 
Successful candidates take oath of office at Oakland Schools Board of Education 
Organizational Meeting.

OAKLAND INTERMEDIATE SCHOOL DISTRICT 
Election Date:  June 4, 2018 
Vote for Two 
Six Year Term Ending June 30, 2024 
Filing Official 
Oakland County Elections Division 
West Wing Extension, 1st Floor 
1200 N. Telegraph Rd., Dept. 417 
Pontiac, MI  48341-0417  
(248) 858-0564 
Qualifications 
A candidate for any seat on the Oakland Schools Board of Education must be a registered voter  at least 
18 years of age,  a citizen of the United States,  and must reside within one of Oakland County's school 
districts. Additionally, a candidate must be a resident of the State of Michigan for at least 30 days; and a 
resident of one of the 28 constituent school districts on or before the 30th day prior to the date of the 
election.  A candidate for the 2018 election may also be currently seated on a local school district board 
of education. 
Nominating Petitions, Filing Fee and Affidavits of Identity 
Friday, May 4, 2018 at 4:00 p.m. is the last day to file petitions with the County Clerk. Petition and 
Affidavit of Identity forms are available at the Oakland County Elections Division, 1200 N. Telegraph Rd., 
Dept. 417, Pontiac 48341-0417. 
A minimum of 40 and no more than 100 valid signatures is required for each candidate. Only qualified 
registered electors of the OAKLAND INTERMEDIATE SCHOOL DISTRICT  may sign nominating petitions.  
Petitions must be circulated on a city/township basis.  Electors may sign petitions for only as many 
candidates as there are persons to be elected to such office. 
Those circulating petitions must be qualifie d registered electors in the State of Michigan . The circulator 
must sign each petition page – care must be taken to date the petition form AFTER it has been 
circulated. The County Clerk is responsible for determining the sufficiency of any petitions filed by OISD 
candidates and the eligibility of the candidates to seek a position on the OISD board. 
A $100 nonrefundable fee may be filed by the candidate in lieu of a petition. 
The statutes which govern the election of OISD board members through the meeting process provide 
that the chairperson of the meeting may accept nominations for an available position “from the floor” if 
no candidates filed for the position.  Consequently, in an instance where no candidates file for an 
available ISD position, the ballot must contain several lines for the hand entry of the candidates who 
receive nomination at the meeting. 
Conduct of Meeting 
The President and Secretary of the OISD are responsible for convening and conducting any meetings 
held by the OISD to fill its board positions; County Clerks are not required to play any role in the conduct 
of the meetings.

Good afternoon,
Two individuals have filed as candidates for the two seats open for the Oakland Schools Board
of Education Biennial Election.
Dr. Theresa Rich (6-year term)
Mr. Marc Katz (6-year term)
Oakland Schools board members are elected by an electoral body composed of one (1) person
designated by the board of each constituent school district. The Revised School Code
prescribes that each constituent school district board must designate a representative to this
electoral body by adopting a resolution that will 1) designate the person to vote in the election
on behalf of the local district board, 2) direct the designee which candidate the board supports
for each position to be filled, 3) direct the designee to vote for those individuals, at least on the
According to the revised school code, districts shall consider the resolution at no less than one
(1) meeting before adopting the resolution. Districts have interpreted this language differently
and historically have used one of the following three options to consider and adopt their
resolutions:
resolution as an action item during another open meeting, with each meeting occurring
on a separate date
resolution as an action item during a second open meeting immediately following the
first open meeting
at the same open meeting (Local school districts selecting this option should seek
independent legal counsel confirming a singular open meeting to consider and adopt a

person at the election scheduled to take place between 5:30 – 7:30 PM on Monday, June 4,
2018 in Conference Room B of the Oakland Schools building located at 2111 Pontiac Lake
Road in Waterford. The revised school code prohibits the acceptance of absentee ballots.
·          Consider the resolution as a discussion item during an open meeting and adopt the

·          Consider the resolution as a discussion item and adopt the resolution as an action item
resolution is compliant with the revised school code).

After your board has adopted its resolution as prescribed by the revised school code,
please fax (248.209.2206) a copy of the resolution to the Oakland Schools’
enable us to compile a list of district designees prior to the election date.
